## Latest planning evidence — AT-343 video follow-up / Manifest 95 / Ledger 103

**26 November 2026: a new owner-supplied measurement video has been received.** Trev asks that the faint X marks be used to investigate a precise reference grid for rebuilding the maps. **Current finding:** the earlier bounded Vert comparison, observed Wild Zone 20 video rows and independently reviewed local image registration support using the crosses as local reference checks. A waypoint value of **60** is visible around **20.9–21.0 seconds**. This is not a count of footsteps or a confirmed metres-per-cell measurement. Partly obscured waypoint-measurement endpoints prevent treating that value as proof of one exact grid interval. The new report controls any numerical image-registration claims; no universal exact-60 spacing, citywide calibration or developer-purpose claim is established.

**Reconstruction requirement:** preserve the common north-up source orientation, establish each location from its buildings and roads, then check nearby cross rows and spacing. Record original source dimensions, hashes, coordinate space, controls and independent checks for every measured transform. A numbered Atlas reference grid may be defined from validated image measurements, but that would be our coordinate convention. Source-image pixels, grid-relative references, native 1920×1920 Atlas pixels and in-game distance must remain distinct until their relationship is verified. The earlier cross-equals-present-item rule remains withdrawn; no item, structure or gameplay pin is approved by a cross alone.

## Latest planning evidence — AT-343 / Manifest 92 / Ledger 100

**Latest correction: the general cross-equals-item rule is unsupported and withdrawn.** Trev subsequently reported finding many crosses with no ball present; their number is unspecified. The marks’ game meaning, real/game units and global origin remain unconfirmed; the bounded relative-alignment result below does not restore an item-marker interpretation. No new pin, native coordinate, collectible identity or runtime behavior is approved by these observations.

**Bounded alignment result:** the strict pilot supports the marks as a **secondary map-relative alignment reference for one Vert photograph pair**, after shared architecture establishes correspondence. It retained **197 candidate feature correspondences, 97 inliers at 82 distinct source locations**—not 82 named landmarks—with **1.732 original-photograph-pixel RMS**. The roof and canal cross holdouts lie **inside the actual architecture-inlier convex hull**, with residuals of **3.565 and 4.284 photograph pixels**. Eastern checks lie outside that hull and are extrapolation diagnostics, with larger residuals of **17.098 and 28.642 pixels**. A separate local HUD comparison supports map-relative rather than fixed-screen anchoring only for this pair. Trev cannot currently circle an individual example and intends to provide before-pickup photos or video; no such new capture or verification is claimed here.

## START HERE — mandatory preflight for every development cycle

**Read these first, in order, before any research or code change:** [Simple completion plan](FINAL_WORKSPACE_PLAN.md) · [Consolidated handoff](ATLAS_RELEASE_HANDOFF.md) · [Master plan](MAPS_SECTOR_UPGRADE_MASTER_PLAN.md) · [Manifest](MAPS_UPGRADE_MANIFEST.json) · [Two-site ledger](ATLAS_DUAL_SITE_FIX_LEDGER.md) · [Access survey](MAPS_ACCESS_ROUTE_SURVEY.json) · private Supabase `atlas_internal.feature_checklist` and `atlas_internal.report_resolution_queue` ([feedback protocol](docs/qa/ATLAS_FEEDBACK_RESOLUTION_TRACKER.md)). Read current main **and** any active development PR at its exact head; do not use stale conversation summaries as authority. Follow linked plans and check current site-specific evidence. At each checkpoint synchronize the plan, manifest, ledger and relevant private checklist rows; keep links/purpose/route step, revision, status and evidence current. GitHub and Workspace remain separate until independently deployed and verified. Never claim a feature is live from a branch or a passing test alone. Preserve existing progress, maps, photographs, feedback and original Workspace. Stop promotion on missing, failed or contradictory evidence. Map source images must follow the [AT-301 JPEG/PNG crop and provenance register](MAPS_BORDERLESS_SOURCE_REGISTER_2026-11-24.json). Before original Site access or changes, read [Workspace access and safe upgrade policy](WORKSPACE_ACCESS_HANDOFF.md#at-297--workspace-source-access-export-and-safe-upgrade-policy).

## Standing original-site preservation and change-mirroring protocol

**Owner instruction — 21 November 2026:** Leave **Edition A, the original Workspace Site**, completely intact and online. Do not edit, export by rebuilding, redeploy, replace, transfer DNS, alter the Supabase connection or reset its saved player progress as a side effect of improving Edition B. **A remains a separate, pending application target** until the original editor/source is actually accessible. This is not permission for blanket fixes or automatic deployment.

**Every proposed correction, improvement, or actual Edition B change must be mirrored in this ledger** so the Workspace can later receive the equivalent fact-checked improvement, **without overwriting features that A already has**. Use the existing stable AT-number when a finding is already tracked; create a new ID only for a genuinely new change. Do not mark proposals as implemented.

| Mandatory per-change evidence | What to record |
| --- | --- |
| **Change identity** | Stable `AT-NNN`, category (**fact correction**, **feature**, **navigation**, **visual polish**, **bug/security fix**), scope, affected pages, original report/QA finding and priority |
| **Why and evidence** | Exact observed B behaviour or source discrepancy, expected player-facing outcome, verified game references, unresolved claims and whether any existing A functionality may already satisfy it |
| **Edition B implementation** | `PLANNED` / `DRAFT PR` / `MERGED` / `DEPLOYED + VERIFIED`, file paths, old/new content summary, GitHub branch, PR, reviewed commit SHA, build/browser/mobile test receipts and rollback point |
| **Edition A Workspace mirror** | `NOT INSPECTED — SOURCE UNAVAILABLE` / `ALREADY PRESENT — VERIFIED` / `NEEDS EQUIVALENT CHANGE` / `APPLIED — UNPUBLISHED` / `PUBLISHED + VERIFIED`; **copy-ready instructions** identifying original page or component, exact text or behaviour to change, dependencies and linked items/map IDs. **Never claim its existing code matches B without inspecting A** |
| **Cross-links, data and compatibility** | Checkboxes and localStorage key preservation, stable item number ↔ map pin linkage, side-quest/main-mission/navigation targets, form/rating integration, mobile and desktop regression, external assets and relevant Supabase origin restrictions |
| **Per-site final sign-off** | Independent A URL and mobile/desktop proof, independent B URL/commit/test proof, saved progress intact, no broken links; only then set `BOTH VERIFIED` and record date. Old feedback #13/#14 stay until both fixes are proven and owner authorizes cleanup |

**Operational rule:** Work on a **separate, reviewable Edition B branch** only after the relevant problem and minimal fix are understood. Record the B implementation immediately, including *what changed and what did not*; then record the **equivalent action pending in A** in plain language the Workspace editor can execute. A change done only in a GitHub draft is **not** a change to either published site. Later, compare A's real original files against B's tested changes **before** choosing which changes A needs; copy improvements rather than wholesale replacing A.

**Workspace access first:** follow [WORKSPACE_ACCESS_HANDOFF.md](WORKSPACE_ACCESS_HANDOFF.md) to request a **read-only export of the actual original Site project** (source, routes, assets, scripts and build config, no credentials). A published-page/MHTML reconstruction is **not** that export. Access/exports do not authorize editing or deploying A.

**Current session:** this section records a standing rule and source-access plan **only**. No guide gameplay code, original Site, Supabase functions/tables, active URLs, draft PRs or reports were modified by this documentation entry.

## Status meanings

- **Observed live** = seen working from screenshot or current running service; does not mean the underlying original source is accessible.
- **Reported / source missing** = owner, prior walkthrough or capture indicates the condition, but its original source/runtime has not been independently inspected.
- **Draft in PR** = changes exist on a named GitHub branch and are **not** merged, published or proven working on users' phones.
- **Blocked / unverified** = do not publish or mark complete until identified checks are done.
- **Both verified** = A and B individually tested after the fix, with recorded date, URL, evidence and branch/Workspace version where available. No row is in this state yet.

### Cross-branch dependency and no-loss rule

- [PR #1](https://github.com/trevieisking/lumiose-atlas-pokemon-za/pull/1): Mable's Research and audit — standalone draft from `main`.
- [PR #2](https://github.com/trevieisking/lumiose-atlas-pokemon-za/pull/2): social share / IONOS plan — standalone draft from `main`.
- [PR #3](https://github.com/trevieisking/lumiose-atlas-pokemon-za/pull/3): missions 01–10 and crosslinks — draft from `main`.
- [PR #4](https://github.com/trevieisking/lumiose-atlas-pokemon-za/pull/4): numbered pins — **based on PR #3's branch**.
- [PR #5](https://github.com/trevieisking/lumiose-atlas-pokemon-za/pull/5): safe progress import — **based on PR #4's branch**.
- Preserve this relationship. Do not independently merge #5 onto `main` or replace staged files. Reconciling #1 and #2 requires collision review.
- No broad refactors, main force-push, automated publishing, Supabase reset, TCG code changes or changes to original Site without verified scope and approval.

## How to register a fix (use one stable ID; never silently mark both complete)

1. Record **reported issue**, source (player/screenshot/guide source), mission/URL and expected behavior. Do not publish personal feedback text in a public repo.
2. Fact-check the change and record gameplay evidence, uncertainty and source. Mark **VERIFIED FACT** only when supported.
3. Record **Edition A:** original Workspace page/edit version, tested URL and mobile/desktop result; else explicitly **NOT ACCESSIBLE / PENDING**.
4. Record **Edition B:** exact GitHub PR, branch and reviewed SHA, static/browser test receipts and deployed URL; else **DRAFT / PENDING**.
5. Mark **BOTH VERIFIED** only after two independent on-site checks and saved progress regression checks.
6. For feedback-backed items (AT-002, AT-003), leave Supabase report rows untouched while either edition is unresolved. After both verified, record results and obtain a new explicit owner instruction before deleting those rows.
7. Add a dated entry to the append-only log below; never erase past status transitions.

## AT-278 — Existing-map location permalinks (23 November 2026)

**Issue/source:** Read-only audit of `atlas-explorer.js` confirmed that a sector can be selected using `?sector=`, but photographed Holovators, the Hotel Z ladder and saved Wild Zone centres have no equivalent direct location URL. This is a navigation/shareability gap, not a map-coordinate defect.

**Scoped fix under separate release gate:** Add `?place=<known atlas-explorer-data.json record ID>` and a "Direct link to this location" anchor on each existing record's evidence panel. Reuse the exact existing record, photos and directions; preserve `?sector=`. Tests must check Mission 61's five owner photos, the Hotel Z route selection, five owner photos for the separate Holovator record near Wild Zone 6 and safe fallback for nonexistent IDs on desktop/phone. No new map pins or cross-site data migration. Preserve AT-267/issue #99 map access, all progress/Supabase feedback, original ChatGPT-hosted Edition A, and no moving DLC pins. **Publication remains contingent on exact-PR-head CI and a separately verified GitHub Pages success**; no PR preview is treated as the owner's live test guide.

## AT-279 - Correct treasure observation and Zygarde no-hit tactics (23 November 2026)

**Owner field correction (not an established game mechanic):** On a later playthrough, valuable treasure pickups felt **less frequent in the late game, NOT worth less per treasure**. Do not invent a lower sale value or a verified late-game spawn-rate modifier. One-time fixed pickups can become exhausted while separate glowing field pickups have been reported to respawn; these different pickup classes may explain the experience, but this has **not** been demonstrated as the cause in the owner's save. [General money / treasures reference](https://www.dexerto.com/wikis/pokemon-legends-z-a/how-to-earn-money-fast/) and [pickup respawn category notes](https://gamepedia.jp/pokemon-legends-z-a/archives/11555).

**Unreproduced occurrence:** On the owner's **second playthrough**, around **October 2026**, an unusual treasure outcome of approximately **120,000 Pokédollars occurred twice**. The owner did not knowingly activate cheats, cannot recreate the sequence, and the present evidence does not identify an item, pickup site, quest transaction, game build, game-save log, bug trigger or exploit. **Do not publish a reproducible treasure glitch or hidden cheat claim.** Keep this as an open player observation awaiting a reliable reproduction or independent corroboration; no game/save data or treasure map dots changed.

**Confirmed guide improvement:** In independent GitHub Edition B, journey.html#journey-mission-42 now describes the **Core Enforcer Z-shaped blast**, early move to the arena's left/right edge, its small unlit triangular safe pockets, the last-resort dodge roll (not guaranteed), and the **separate** inward-moving green-ring attack. Sources: [Nintendo Life boss route](https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/pokemon-legends-z-a-how-to-catch-zygarde), [Bulbapedia Part 29](https://bulbapedia.bulbagarden.net/wiki/Walkthrough%3APok%C3%A9mon_Legends%3A_Z-A/Part_29), [player safe-pocket discussions](https://www.reddit.com/r/PokemonZA/comments/1qoq1ft/how_do_i_dodge_the_final_form_of_zygardes_weird/). ### AT-295 owner enhancement — smart video return (24 November 2026)

The owner requested that opening a video remember exactly where the player was in the guide. Native timestamped links still open a separate tab/app; on explicit activation the Atlas Explorer saves one versioned navigation bookmark under `lumiose-atlas:video-return:v1` and the current browser history entry. This is a narrow owner-approved exception to the earlier no-storage rule, solely for local video-return navigation. Restore route and instruction step, selected location, map pan/zoom/rotation/tint, filters/search, visible layers, coverage selection and scroll relative to the clicked link. Plain Explorer reopening resumes the last saved step on the same origin/device, while explicit place/sector/hash links take precedence unless returning to the same saved history entry. Stale route/step/video identities and malformed data are ignored. If browser storage is blocked, native video navigation remains usable. A Forget saved video place button clears only this bookmark and current history state, not any progress keys. No playback tracking, account synchronization, third-party SDK, backend, original Workspace, collection, map asset or marker changes. Existing 12 routes, 21 approximate Holovators and 111 original photos unchanged. New unit tests cover bookmark validation, unrelated storage preservation and blocked storage; browser tests cover actual outbound click (intercepted offline), popup, reload, map/step restoration, fresh tab, explicit deep link, clearing, mobile width and desktop. Earlier video-only CI run 45414225060 passed at aa1794e6caeb0c8efba45093e8896dfdb8bdce25; this new head requires its own CI and visual QA. Manifest rev38. Continue Vert reconciliation after validated release.

### AT-333 — Compact collectible dots and independent filters (25 November 2026)

Owner requires hundreds of item markers to appear as very small balls, not large map icons, and to be independently filterable like the other layers. This is a binding SM-06 and AT-325 G4/G5 acceptance requirement, not an authorization to plot unregistered source coordinates.

- **Compact symbols:** fixed outdoor item pickups and Colorful Screws use distinct small solid circular dots, not the existing 40-CSS-pixel major-location markers. Proposed initial visual target: 6–8 CSS px diameter at overview and normal zoom, with a restrained 10–12 px selected outline; inspect on phone and desktop and adjust for readability. Keep the dot visually small using existing inverse-zoom behavior; no full-time item names or number labels on the map. Distinct item/Screw colours and a labelled legend; colour is not the only way to identify an item.
- **Independent layers:** separate Items and Colorful Screws checkboxes in Visible map layers, capable of being enabled individually, together, or hidden independently of all other existing layers. Proposed initial default is off for both to preserve the uncluttered map. Search/category filters and existing directory selection behavior remain available; hide only the intended category, preserve selection/zoom/rotation and other layer choices. Empty verified layers explicitly report no verified points rather than implying that the city contains none.
- **Accessible details:** select a dot or existing numbered directory/search result to open its name, stable original item/Screw ID, quantity where known, elevation/scene, evidence status, unlocks and written access directions, with available photos. Small visual size must not mean an unusably small touch target: use proximity selection or nonoverlapping hit areas and accessible list/keyboard controls; where points coincide or lie close, offer a chooser with every matching record and its floor. Do not move plotted dots away from verified coordinates or merge different pickup identities.
- **Progress and evidence:** retain original collection keys and read existing progress rather than creating a second checklist or resetting collected state. Optional hide-collected view only if safely supported by existing progress ownership; filtering must never mark items collected. Use only verified fixed pickups in the correct map scene; no dots for uncertain source XY, broad area hints, gifts, scripted rewards, random drops, transient Hyperspace or invented positions. Unverified entries remain in their existing directory, with uncertainty intact.
- **Scale validation:** test hundreds of eligible points using synthetic fixtures confined to tests (at least 364 distinct records; not a claim that all 264 current item records plus 100 Screws are fixed outdoor pickups). Test both collectible toggles in all four combinations, mixed layers, search, touch, keyboard and selection/overlap, pan/pinch/two-finger rotation/desktop controls/north reset, deep links and stable collection keys. Inspect actual phone/desktop screenshots at overview and zoom. No performance regression or obstruction of major location markers; exclude invalid coordinates and random content; prove production data contains no synthetic coordinates. Use existing QA and separate GitHub and Workspace verification gates.

Baseline inspection: `atlas-explorer.css` uses 40px existing markers and inverse-zoom sizing; `atlas-explorer.js` has separate layer visibility and search categories but no fixed item/Screw pin records; `items-data.js` records 264 original item entries, 100 stable Screw IDs and zero confirmed item/Screw pins. Preserve 146 approximate item leads, 70 route research leads, all existing map/progress assets, and AT-332's 12 uncalibrated source references. No runtime changes in this checkpoint; implementation and test evidence remain pending coordinate verification and G1–G5. Manifest revision 78; ledger revision 86.
